\displaystyle\langle\partial^{m}(v\cdot\nabla M),\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{m}(v\cdot\nabla M)\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|v\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{H^{s}_{\rho}}\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}\,,

and

(3.56)

\displaystyle-\lambda\mu_{0}\langle\partial^{m}H_{ext},\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle\lesssim\|\partial^{m}H_{ext}\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}\,,

and

(3.57)

\displaystyle-\langle\partial^{m}(\Gamma(M)M),\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle

\displaystyle=-\langle\partial^{m}\left[(2A\lambda|\nabla M|^{2}+\lambda\mu_{0}H_{ext}\cdot M)M\right],\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\|\partial^{m}(|\nabla M|^{2}M)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial^{m}((M\cdot H_{ext})M)\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[(1+\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}})\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(1+\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}^{2})\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\right]\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}\,,

and

(3.58)

\displaystyle\gamma\langle\partial^{m}\left[M\times(2A\Delta M+\mu_{0}H_{ext})\right],\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\gamma A\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}M\times\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}M,\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle+\gamma\mu_{0}\langle\partial^{m}(M\times H_{ext}),\Delta\partial^{m}M\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}M\times\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}M\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial^{m}(M\times H_{ext})\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}+(1+\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}})\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(1+\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}})\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}]\right\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Substituting the relations (
3.54
), (
3.55
), (
3.56
), (
3.57
), and (
3.58
) into (
3.53
) yields

(3.59)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla\partial^{m}M\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2A\lambda\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|v\|_{H^{s}_{\rho}}\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left[(1+\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}})\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(1+\|\nabla M\|_{H^{s}}^{2})\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\right]\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)
in (
1.10
), the estimate (
3.59
) implies the energy bound (
3.52
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

3.1.6.
Closing the estimates: Proof of Proposition
3.1

Using Lemmas
3.1
,
3.2
,
3.3
,
3.4
and
3.5
, we now close the local a priori estimate (
3.1
), thereby proving Proposition
3.1
. From (
3.2
), (
3.13
), (
3.18
) and (
3.52
), we have

(3.60)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\|\partial^{m}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{\rho}}^{2}+\|\partial^{m}F\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial^{m}v\|_{L^{2}_{\rho}}^{2}+\|\nabla\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mu\|\nabla\partial^{m}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)+\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)

\displaystyle+\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(t)+\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{H^{s-1}}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)

\displaystyle+\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\big]\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)

\displaystyle+\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)+\left[(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t))\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\right]\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\,.

Substituting (
3.41
) into (
3.60
) yields

(3.61)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\|\partial^{m}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{\rho}}^{2}+\|\partial^{m}F\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial^{m}v\|_{L^{2}_{\rho}}^{2}+\|\nabla\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mu\|\nabla\partial^{m}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\Delta\partial^{m}M\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)+\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)+\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{2}(t)

\displaystyle+(1+\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}})(\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|^{\frac{s+1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}})\big(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{s+\gamma}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{s+2}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\big)\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)

\displaystyle+\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\big]\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)

\displaystyle+\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)+\left[(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}(t))\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}}\right]\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\,.

Recall the definitions of the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)
and the energy dissipation rate functional
\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)
in (
1.10
). Summing over
|m|\leq s
in (
3.61
) therefore implies that

(3.62)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}})(\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{s+1}{2}})\big[\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{s+\gamma+3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{s+5}{2}}(t)\big]

\displaystyle+(1+\|H_{ext}\|_{H^{s}})(1+\|\tfrac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{s+1}{2}})\big[1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(t)\big]\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\,.

It remains to control the quantity
\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}
. Recall that the density
\rho
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho+v\cdot\nabla\rho+\rho\nabla\cdot v=0\,,

with initial data
\rho|_{t=0}=\rho_{0}
. Define the trajectory map

\begin{cases}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}X(t,x)=v(t,X(t,x))\,,\\
X(0,x)=x\,.\end{cases}

Then the density
\rho
along the trajectory map
X(t,x)
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho(t,X(t,x)))+(\nabla\cdot v)(t,X(t,x))\rho(t,X(t,x))=0\,,

which implies that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big[e^{\int^{t}_{0}(\nabla\cdot v)(\tau,X(\tau,x))d\tau}\rho(t,X(t,x))\Big]=0\,.

Integrating the above equation over
[0,t]
gives

\displaystyle\rho(t,X(t,x))=\rho_{0}(x)e^{-\int^{t}_{0}(\nabla\cdot v)(\tau,X(\tau,x))d\tau}\,,

which shows that
\rho(t,X(t,x))>0
provided that the initial data satisfies
\rho_{0}(x)>0
. Consequently, we have

(3.63)

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}e^{\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\cdot v\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau}\leq\|\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}e^{c\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla v\|_{H^{2}}d\tau}\leq\|\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}}e^{ct\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}

for some constant
c>0
. As a result, (
3.62
) together with (
3.63
) yields the inequality (
3.1
). This completes the proof of Proposition
3.1
.

3.2.
Local well-posedness of (
1.8
)

In this subsection, based on the energy estimate (
3.1
) in Proposition
3.1
, we establish the local existence and uniqueness of solutions to the compressible Navier-Stokes-Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert system (
1.8
) for large initial data.

We first construct the following nonlinear iterative approximation scheme:

(3.64)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho^{n+1}+v^{n}\cdot\nabla\rho^{n+1}+\rho^{n+1}\nabla\cdot v^{n}=0,\\[4.0pt]
\rho^{n}(\partial_{t}v^{n+1}+v^{n}\cdot\nabla v^{n+1})+P^{\prime}(\rho^{n})\nabla\rho^{n+1}+\nabla(-A|\nabla M^{n}|^{2}+\mu_{0}M^{n}\cdot H_{ext})\\
=\mu\Delta v^{n+1}+(\mu+\xi)\nabla(\nabla\cdot v^{n+1})+\nabla\cdot(\rho^{n}F^{n}F^{n\top})\\
\qquad\qquad-2A\nabla\cdot(\nabla M^{n}\odot\nabla M^{n})+\mu_{0}(\nabla H_{ext})^{\top}M^{n},\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{t}F^{n+1}+v^{n}\cdot\nabla F^{n+1}=\nabla v^{n}F^{n},\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{t}M^{n+1}+v^{n}\cdot\nabla M^{n+1}=2A\lambda\Delta M^{n+1}+\lambda\mu_{0}H_{ext}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Gamma(M^{n+1})M^{n+1}-\gamma M^{n+1}\times(\Delta M^{n+1}+H_{ext}),\\[4.0pt]
\left|M^{n+1}\right|=1,\Gamma(M^{n+1})=2A\lambda|\nabla M^{n+1}|^{2}-\lambda\mu_{0}M^{n+1}\cdot H_{ext}.\end{cases}

The initial data are given by

\displaystyle(\rho^{n+1},v^{n+1},F^{n+1},M^{n+1})|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},v_{0},F_{0},M_{0})(x)\,.

The iteration starts from
n=0
with

\displaystyle(\rho^{0},v^{0},F^{0},M^{0})(t,x)=(\rho_{0},v_{0},F_{0},M_{0})(x)\,.

The iteration for the compressible variables
(\rho^{n+1},v^{n+1})
is inspired by (5.1) in Section 5 of
[
11
]
. The iteration for the deformation gradient tensor
F^{n+1}
and the magnetization
M^{n+1}
follows the approach in (4.1) of Section 4 of
[
9
]
. The nonlinearity in the iterative scheme arises from the iterated Lagrangian multiplier
\Gamma(M^{n+1})
, which enforces the geometric constraint
|M^{n+1}|=1
. This nonlinear iterative scheme for preserving the spherical constraint was first introduced in
[
8
]
for the incompressible hyperbolic Ericksen–Leslie model of liquid crystals. Subsequently, the ideas were successfully applied in
[
9
,
11
]
.

Following the ideas of
[
9
,
8
,
11
]
, the iterative approximation (
3.64
) admits a uniform estimate in
n\geq 0
that has essentially the same structure as the local a priori estimate (
3.1
) in Proposition
3.1
. Using this uniform estimate, the local well-posedness can be obtained. For simplicity, we will directly work with the local a priori estimate (
3.1
).

Under the assumption that
H_{\text{ext}}\in L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H^{s})
for some
T_{0}>0
as in Theorem
1.1
, the bound (
3.1
) implies that

(3.65)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)\leq C(1+e^{c(s+1)t\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\mathcal{E}_{s}(\tau)})(1+\mathcal{E}_{s}^{\frac{s}{2}+\gamma+3}(t))

for all
t\in[0,T_{0}]
, where the constant
C=C(s,\|H_{ext}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H^{s})},\|\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}})>0
. Recall that
\mathcal{E}_{s}(0)=\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}<\infty
, where the local initial energy
\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}
is defined in (
1.11
). Define

(3.66)

\displaystyle T_{1}=\sup\{\tau\in[0,T_{0});\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}\}\geq 0\,.

By the continuity of the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)
, we have
T_{1}>0
. Then inequality (
3.65
) yields, for all
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)\leq\underbrace{C\left(1+e^{2c(s+1)T_{0}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}}\right)\left(1+(2\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in})^{\frac{s}{2}+\gamma+3}\right)}_{C_{1}}.

Integrating this inequality over
[0,t]\subseteq[0,T_{1}]
and using the non-negativity of
\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)
, we obtain

(3.67)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\leq\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}+C_{1}t.

Choosing

t_{*}=\frac{\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}}{2C_{1}}>0,

which depends only on
s
, the coefficients,
\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}
and
\|H_{ext}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H^{s})}
, it follows from (
3.67
) that

\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}<2\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}\quad(\forall t\in[0,t_{*}]).

Consequently, the number
T_{1}
defined in (
3.66
) admits a uniform positive lower bound
t_{*}>0
; i.e.,

T_{1}\geq t_{*}>0.

Hence, for all
t\in[0,t_{*}]
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)\leq C_{1},

which implies

\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}\mathcal{E}_{s}(t)+\int_{0}^{t_{*}}\mathcal{D}_{s}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\mathcal{E}_{s}^{in}+C_{1}t_{*}.

This establishes the local existence of solutions to the compressible NSLLG system (
1.8
). The uniqueness result in the classical solution regime is standard; for instance, one may refer to Section 4 of
[
10
]
. Thus the proof of Theorem
1.1
is complete.

4.
Global well-posedness near constant equilibrium: Proof of Theorem
1.2

In this section, we establish the global well-posedness of the compressible NSLLG system (
1.8
) without external magnetic field (
H_{{ext}}=0
) near the constant equilibrium
(1,0,I,M_{e})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
. More precisely, we prove the global existence of solutions to (
1.12
) for small initial data. We first examine the damping mechanism of the deformation gradient tensor
F
near the identity matrix
I\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
. Then we derive global differential energy estimates to obtain the global existence result.

4.1.
Damping mechanism of the deformation gradient tensor
F
in (
1.12
)

From the initial assumptions
\rho_{0}>0
and
\rho_{0}\det F_{0}=1
, Proposition 1 of Qian-Zhang
[
15
]
implies that for all
(t,x)
,

\displaystyle\rho\det F=1\,,\quad\rho>0\,.

Thus
F
is nondegenerate. Let
G=F^{-1}
. Then

(\partial_{t}F+u\cdot\nabla F)G=-F(\partial_{t}G+u\cdot\nabla G),

and

\nabla uFG=\nabla u.

Consequently,

-F(\partial_{t}G+u\cdot\nabla G)=\nabla u,

i.e.,

\partial_{t}G+u\cdot\nabla G+G\nabla u=0.

Moreover, by Lemma 2.1 of
[
51
]
, we have
\partial_{i}G^{jk}=\partial_{k}G^{ji}
for
i,j,k=1,2,3
. Define
U=G-I
. Then
G
satisfies

(4.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U+u\cdot\nabla U+(U+I)\nabla u=0\,,

and
\partial_{i}U^{jk}=\partial_{k}U^{ji}\quad(i,j,k=1,2,3)
, that is, the matrix
U
is curl-free. More precisely, let
U(t,x)=(\mathscr{U}^{1}(t,x),\mathscr{U}^{2}(t,x),\mathscr{U}^{3}(t,x))^{\top}
, where

\mathscr{U}^{j}(t,x)=(U^{j1}(t,x),U^{j2}(t,x),U^{j3}(t,x))\quad(j=1,2,3).

Then each vector field
\mathscr{U}^{j}
is curl-free:
\nabla\times\mathscr{U}^{j}=0
for
j=1,2,3
. By the div-curl theorem, there exists an
\mathbb{R}^{3}
-valued function
\psi(t,x)=(\psi^{1}(t,x),\psi^{2}(t,x),\psi^{3}(t,x))
such that

(U^{j1},U^{j2},U^{j3})=\nabla\psi^{j}\quad(j=1,2,3).

In other words,
U(t,x)=\nabla\psi(t,x)
, whose components are given by

(4.2)

\displaystyle U^{ij}=\partial_{i}\psi^{i}\,.

Note that
\psi(t,x)=X^{-1}(t,x)-x
, where
X^{-1}(t,x)
is the inverse flow map
x(X,t)
defined in (
1.2
).

Substituting (
4.2
) into (
4.1
) yields

(4.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{j}(\partial_{t}\psi^{i}+u^{i}+(u\cdot\nabla)\psi^{i})=0\,,\quad 1\leq i,j\leq 3\,.

By (3.3) of
[
14
]
, we have

\|U(t)\|_{H^{s}}=\|F^{-1}(t)-I\|_{H^{s}}\sim\|F(t)-I\|_{H^{s}},

provided that the fluctuation satisfies
\|F(t)-I\|_{H^{s}}<1
(or equivalently
\|F^{-1}(t)-I\|_{H^{s}}<1
). By the smallness of the initial data, there exists
T>0
such that
\|F(t)-I\|_{H^{s}}<1
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Then, using Taylor expansion, the Cauchy–Green tensor becomes

(4.4)

\displaystyle\frac{W_{F}(F)F^{\top}}{\det F}

\displaystyle=\rho FF^{\top}=(1+\theta)(I+U)^{-1}(I+U)^{-\top}

\displaystyle=(1+\theta)(I-U-U^{\top}+g(U))\,,

where
g(U)=O(|U|^{2}).

Using the curl-free property
\partial_{i}U^{jk}=\partial_{k}U^{ji}\ (1\leq i,j,k\leq 3)
, we obtain
\nabla\cdot U^{\top}=\nabla\mathrm{tr}U.
Hence,

\nabla\cdot(\rho FF^{\top})=\nabla\theta-\nabla\cdot((1+\theta)\nabla\psi)-\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta-(1+\theta)\nabla\mathrm{tr}U.

Since
\rho\det F=1
, we have

1+\theta=\det(I+U)=1+\mathrm{tr}U+O(|U|^{2}).

Thus
\mathrm{tr}U=\theta+\tilde{g}(U),\ \tilde{g}(U)=O(|U|^{2})
. Consequently,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot(\rho FF^{\top})

\displaystyle=\nabla\theta-\nabla\cdot\left((1+\theta)\nabla\psi\right)-\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta-(1+\theta)\nabla\left(\theta+\tilde{g}(U)\right)

\displaystyle=-\nabla\cdot\left((1+\theta)\nabla\psi\right)-\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta-\nabla\tilde{g}(U)-\theta\nabla\left(\theta+\tilde{g}(U)\right)

\displaystyle=-(1+\theta)\Delta\psi-2\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta-\nabla\tilde{g}(U)-\theta\nabla\left(\theta+\tilde{g}(U)\right)\,.

From (
1.12
), (
4.3
) and (
4.5
), we deduce the following reformulated system:

(4.6)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla\theta+(1+\theta)\nabla\cdot u=0\,,\\
\begin{aligned} (1&+\theta)(\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u)+\nabla\left(P(1+\theta)-A|\nabla d|^{2}+\tilde{g}(U)\right)\\[5.0pt]
&=\mu\Delta u+(\mu+\xi)\nabla(\nabla\cdot u)-(1+\theta)\Delta\psi-2\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta-\theta\nabla(\theta+\tilde{g}(U))-2A\nabla\cdot(\nabla d\odot\nabla d)\,,\end{aligned}\\
\partial_{t}\psi+u+u\cdot\nabla\psi=0\,,\\
\partial_{t}d+u\cdot\nabla d=2A\lambda\Delta d+2A\lambda|\nabla d|^{2}(d+M_{e})-2A\gamma(d+M_{e})\times\Delta d\,,\\
|d|^{2}+2M_{e}\cdot d=0\,.\end{cases}

4.2.
Global existence near constant equilibrium: Proof of Theorem
1.2

Recall the fluctuations

(4.7)

\displaystyle\rho=1+\theta\,,\ M=d+M_{e}\,,\ v=0+u\,,\ U=F^{-1}-I\,,\ U^{ij}=\partial_{j}\psi^{i}\,.

The global energy functional
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
in (
1.13
) and the global dissipation rate
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
in (
1.14
) can be expressed as

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)=\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+|\bar{d}|^{2}+\|d-\bar{d}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\,,

and

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)=\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\psi|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\Delta d\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\cdot u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\,.

For any
\delta,\eta>0
, we define the following
instant energy functional

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon a\gamma\|\theta\|_{H_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon\|u\|_{H_{1+\theta}^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon\eta\|u+\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}-\epsilon\eta\|u\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}-\epsilon\eta\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+|\bar{d}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\delta\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\delta\|u-\bar{u}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}-\frac{2\delta}{\mu}\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi}),\partial^{m}u\rangle\,,

and the
instant dissipation rate

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=(\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu})\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon\eta a\gamma\|\nabla\theta\|_{H_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\mu}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}-(\epsilon+\delta)\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\nabla\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\partial^{m}u\rangle-\frac{\mu+\xi}{\mu}\delta\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}u\rangle

\displaystyle-c_{0}\epsilon\eta[\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}]\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}

\displaystyle-c_{1}\delta a\gamma(\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}+2A\lambda\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\Delta d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\,,

where
\mathfrak{w}(\theta)
satisfies
a\gamma\mathfrak{w}(\theta)=\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}
, hence,

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}(\theta)=(1+\theta)^{\gamma-2}\,.

Here the notations
\bar{d}
,
\bar{u}
and
\bar{\psi}
are given in (
1.16
). Moreover, the constant
c_{0}>0
is independent of
\delta,\eta,\epsilon
.

We then have the following lemma concerning the positivity of the functionals
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
and
\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
.

Lemma 4.1
.

If the coefficients satisfy

(4.13)

\displaystyle a>0,\ \gamma>1,\ A>0,\ \lambda>0,\ \mu>0,\ \mu+\xi>0\,,

then there exist
\delta,\eta,\epsilon>0
such that

(4.14)

\displaystyle c_{1}\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\leq\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\leq\hat{c_{1}}\mathbf{D}_{s}(t),\ c_{2}\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)\leq\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\leq\hat{c_{2}}\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},\hat{c_{1}},\hat{c_{2}}>0
.

Proof.

The upper bounds for the functionals
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
and
\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
in (
4.14
) follow readily from Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality. We now focus on the lower bounds.

For
\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
, we need to find some
\delta,\eta,\epsilon>0
such that the quantity

(4.15)

\displaystyle(\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu})\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon\eta a\gamma\|\nabla\theta\|_{H_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\mu}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}-(\epsilon+\delta)\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\nabla\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\partial^{m}u\rangle-\frac{\mu+\xi}{\mu}\delta\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}u\rangle

\displaystyle-c_{0}\epsilon\eta[\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}]\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}

\displaystyle-c_{1}\delta a\gamma(\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}

admits a lower bound of the form
c_{*}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\cdot u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}^{2})
for some
c_{*}>0
. It suffices to show that the quadratic form

\displaystyle F(V)=V\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu}&0&-\frac{1+\delta}{2}&-\frac{1}{2}(c_{0}\epsilon\eta\mu+c_{1}\delta\mu a\gamma)\\
0&(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)&-\delta\frac{\mu+\xi}{2\mu}&-\frac{1}{2}c_{0}\epsilon\eta(\mu+\xi)\\
-\frac{1+\delta}{2}&-\delta\frac{\mu+\xi}{2\mu}&\frac{\delta}{\mu}&-\frac{1}{2}(c_{0}\epsilon\eta+c_{1}\delta a\gamma)\\
-\frac{1}{2}(c_{0}\epsilon\eta\mu+c_{1}\delta\mu a\gamma)&-\frac{1}{2}c_{0}\epsilon\eta(\mu+\xi)&-\frac{1}{2}(c_{0}\epsilon\eta+c_{1}\delta a\gamma)&\epsilon\eta a\gamma\end{pmatrix}}_{:=\mathcal{M}}V^{\top}

is positive definite, where

\displaystyle V=(X,Y,Z,W)\,,\ X=\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\,,Y=\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}\,,Z=\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}\,,W=\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}\,.

We remark that the quadratic form
F(V)
provides a lower bound for the quantity (
4.15
). The matrix
\mathcal{M}
is positive definite if and only if the following conditions hold:

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}=\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu}>0\,,\quad\mathcal{M}_{2}=\det\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu}&0\\
0&(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)\end{pmatrix}>0\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}=\det\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu}&0&-\frac{1+\delta}{2}\\
0&(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)&-\delta\frac{\mu+\xi}{2\mu}\\
-\frac{1+\delta}{2}&-\delta\frac{\mu+\xi}{2\mu}&\frac{\delta}{\mu}\end{pmatrix}>0\,,\quad\mathcal{M}_{4}=\det\mathcal{M}>0\,.

Observe that
\mathcal{M}_{1}>0
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}>0
imply

(4.17)

\displaystyle\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu}>0\,,\ \mu+\xi>0\,.

Moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{3}>0
and
\mathcal{M}_{4}>0
can be computed as

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}=(\mu+\xi)\Big\{[(1-\frac{c_{p}}{\mu^{2}})(1-\frac{\mu+\xi}{4})-\frac{1}{4}]\delta^{3}+[\epsilon(2-\frac{c_{p}}{\mu^{2}}-\frac{\mu+\xi}{4})-\frac{3}{4}]\delta^{2}

\displaystyle+(\epsilon^{2}-\frac{3}{4})\delta-\frac{1}{4}\Big\}>0\,,

and

(4.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}=\frac{(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)}{\mu}(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p})(\Xi_{2}\eta^{2}+\Xi_{1}\eta+\Xi_{0})>0\,,

where

\displaystyle\Xi_{2}

\displaystyle=c_{0}^{2}\epsilon^{2}\left[-\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\mu^{3}}{\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}}+\frac{\mu+\xi}{\epsilon+\delta}\right)U-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mu^{2}(1+\delta)}{4\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}+\frac{\delta(\mu+\xi)}{4\mu(\epsilon+\delta)}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\left[U\left(a\gamma-\frac{\mu^{3}c_{0}c_{1}\delta a\gamma}{2\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-2c_{0}c_{1}\delta a\gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mu^{2}(1+\delta)}{4\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mu^{2}(1+\delta)}{4\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}+\frac{\delta(\mu+\xi)}{4\mu(\epsilon+\delta)}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\Xi_{0}

\displaystyle=\left(c_{1}\delta a\gamma\right)^{2}\left[\frac{\mu^{3}U}{4\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mu^{2}(1+\delta)}{4\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mu}-\frac{\mu(1+\delta)^{2}}{4\left(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p}\right)}-\frac{\delta^{2}(\mu+\xi)}{4\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)}.

To guarantee (
4.19
), it is sufficient to require

(4.20)

\displaystyle\Xi_{0}>0\,,

which yields (
4.19
) by finally taking
\eta>0
sufficiently small.

We choose
\epsilon\gg 1
and
0<\delta\ll 1
such that
\epsilon^{2}\delta\gg 1
. It is easy to see that (
4.17
) holds, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}>0
. Moreover, to verify (
4.18
), it suffices to show

(4.21)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}\delta>(\frac{c_{p}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{\mu+\xi}{4})\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}\epsilon^{2}\delta+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3}{4}\delta^{2}-[(1-\frac{c_{p}}{\mu^{2}})(1-\frac{\mu+\xi}{4})-\frac{1}{4}]\delta^{3}+\frac{3}{4}\delta\,.

Observe that under the choices
\epsilon\gg 1
,
0<\delta\ll 1
and
\epsilon^{2}\delta\gg 1
, we have
0<\bigl(\frac{c_{p}}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{\mu+\xi}{4}\bigr)\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}\ll 1
and

\Bigl|\frac{3}{4}\delta^{2}-\Bigl[\Bigl(1-\frac{c_{p}}{\mu^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigl(1-\frac{\mu+\xi}{4}\Bigr)-\frac{1}{4}\Bigr]\delta^{3}+\frac{3}{4}\delta\Bigr|\ll 1.

Consequently, (
4.21
) holds, which implies (
4.18
).

We now turn to (
4.19
). We first verify (
4.20
), which can be equivalently written as

\displaystyle\mu^{2}\epsilon+\mu^{2}(1+3\delta)>c_{p}\mu^{2}\delta+\frac{\mu(\mu+\xi)}{4}\frac{\delta^{2}}{\epsilon+\delta}\,.

Since
\mu>0
, the choices
\epsilon\gg 1
,
0<\delta\ll 1
and
\epsilon^{2}\delta\gg 1
imply the above inequality, hence
\Xi_{0}>0
. Then there exists a small
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(\delta,\epsilon)>0
such that

\displaystyle\Xi_{2}\eta^{2}+\Xi_{1}\eta+\Xi_{0}>0

for all
0<\eta<\eta_{0}
. Note that
\frac{(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)}{\mu}(\mu^{2}(\epsilon+\delta)-\delta c_{p})>0
. Thus (
4.19
) follows immediately, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}_{4}>0
.

Therefore, under the choices
\epsilon\gg 1
,
0<\delta\ll 1
and
\epsilon^{2}\delta\gg 1
, the quadratic form
F(V)
is positive definite. In other words, there exists a constant
c_{*}>0
such that

F(V)\geq c_{*}|V|^{2}\quad\text{for all }V=(X,Y,Z,W)\in\mathbb{R}^{4}.

As a consequence,

(4.22)

\displaystyle(\epsilon\mu+\delta\mu-\delta\frac{c_{p}}{\mu})\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(\epsilon+\delta)(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon\eta a\gamma\|\nabla\theta\|_{H_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\mu}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}-(\epsilon+\delta)\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\nabla\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\partial^{m}u\rangle-\frac{\mu+\xi}{\mu}\delta\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}u\rangle

\displaystyle-c_{0}\epsilon\eta[\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}]\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}

\displaystyle-c_{1}\delta a\gamma(\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}

\displaystyle\geq c_{*}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\cdot u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}^{2})\,,

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle\geq c_{*}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\cdot u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}^{2})+2A\lambda\left(\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\Delta d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}\left(\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\Delta d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{1}\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,,

where
c_{1}=\min\left\{c_{*},2A\lambda,\inf_{(t,x)}\mathfrak{w}(\theta)\right\}>0
.

Next we establish the lower bound of
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
. Recall the definition of
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta}(t)
in (
4.10
). For small
\delta>0
and large
\epsilon>0
, we require

(4.23)

\displaystyle\epsilon\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}+\delta\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}-\frac{2\delta}{\mu}\sum_{|m|\leq s}\left\langle\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi}),\partial^{m}u\right\rangle\geq c_{\#}\left(\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}^{2}\right).

Note that

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi}),\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi}),\partial^{m}(u-\bar{u})+\partial^{m}\bar{u}\right\rangle=\left\langle\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi}),\partial^{m}(u-\bar{u})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi})\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\partial^{m}(u-\bar{u})\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq c_{p}\left\|\nabla\partial^{m}\psi\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\partial^{m}(u-\bar{u})\right\|_{L^{2}}\,,

where
c_{p}>0
is the Poincaré constant. Hence,

\displaystyle\delta\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}-\frac{2\delta}{\mu}\sum_{|m|\leq s}\left\langle\partial^{m}(\psi-\bar{\psi}),\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\delta\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}+\epsilon\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}-\frac{2\delta c_{p}}{\mu}\sum_{|m|\leq s}\|\nabla\partial^{m}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{m}(u-\bar{u})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\delta\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}+\epsilon\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}-\frac{4\delta c_{p}}{\mu}\sup_{t,x}(1+\theta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}\,.

Thus we can choose

(4.24)

\displaystyle c_{\#}=\min\{\frac{\delta}{2},\epsilon-\frac{4c_{p}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\delta\}>0

for large
\epsilon\gg 1
and small
0<\delta\ll 1
, which ensures that (
4.23
) holds.

Furthermore, we may select a small
\eta_{0}^{\prime}\leq\eta_{0}
such that for all
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}^{\prime}]
,

\displaystyle\epsilon a\gamma\|\theta\|_{H_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}^{s}}^{2}+c_{\#}\left(\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{s}_{1+\theta}}^{2}\right)+\epsilon\eta\|u+\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}-\epsilon\eta\|u\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}-\epsilon\eta\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}^{\prime}\left(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right)

for some constant
c_{2}^{\prime}>0
. Consequently,

(4.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}^{\prime}\left(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right)+|\bar{d}|^{2}+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\left(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+|\bar{d}|^{2}+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{2}\mathbf{E}_{s}(t),

where
c_{2}=\min\{c_{2}^{\prime},1\}>0
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Based on Lemma
4.1
, we establish the following global energy estimates associated with the instant energy functional
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
in (
4.10
) and instant dissipation rate
\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
in (
4.11
).

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer and let
(\theta,u,\psi,d)
be the solution constructed in Theorem
1.1
with the fluctuation form (
4.7
). Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.1
, we have

(4.26)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)+\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{\frac{s}{2}}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t))\mathbb{E}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)

for some constant
C>0
.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
will be given in the next section. We now establish the global existence of solutions to (
4.6
) (or equivalently (
1.12
)) for small initial data by employing the estimate (
4.26
).

Global well-posedness: Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Assume that
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(0)\leq\varepsilon_{1}
, where the small
\varepsilon_{1}>0
is to be determined later. Define

T^{*}=\sup\left\{t>0:\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}C\left(1+\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{s}{2}}(\tau)\right)\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{2}\right\},

where the constant
C>0
appearing in Proposition
4.1
. We now choose
\varepsilon_{1}>0
so that

C\left(1+\varepsilon_{1}^{\frac{s}{2}}\right)\varepsilon_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

For instance, we may take
\varepsilon_{1}=\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{64C^{2}}\right\}>0
, which satisfies the above inequality. Then we have

C\left(1+\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{s}{2}}(0)\right)\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\leq\frac{1}{4}<\frac{1}{2}.

By the continuity of the instant energy functional
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)
, we obtain
T^{*}>0
. Consequently, the estimate (
4.26
) in Proposition
4.1
yields

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\leq 0\quad\text{for }t\in[0,T^{*}],

which implies

\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\leq\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(0)\leq\varepsilon_{1}\quad\forall t\in[0,T^{*}].

Hence,

\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}C\left(1+\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{s}{2}}(t)\right)\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4}<\frac{1}{2}.

We claim that
T^{*}=\infty
. Indeed, if
T^{*}<\infty
, then by continuity there exists a small
\varepsilon_{2}>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}+\varepsilon_{2}]}C\left(1+\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\leq\frac{3}{8}<\frac{1}{2},

which contradicts the definition of
T^{*}
. Therefore,

(4.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{D}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(0)

provided that
\mathbb{E}_{s;\delta,\eta,\epsilon}(0)\leq\varepsilon_{1}
with
\varepsilon_{1}=\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{64C^{2}}\right\}>0
.
Applying (
4.14
) from Lemma
4.1
to (
4.27
) gives

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\mathfrak{C}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{in}

provided that
\mathbf{E}_{s}(0)=\mathbf{E}_{s}^{in}\leq\varepsilon_{0}=\hat{c}_{2}\varepsilon_{1}
, where
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
is defined in (
1.13
) (or (
4.8
)),
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
is defined in (
1.14
) (or (
4.9
)),
\mathbf{E}_{s}^{in}
is defined in (
1.15
), and the constant

\mathfrak{C}=\min\left\{c_{1},c_{2}\right\}^{-1}\hat{c}_{2}>0.

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
∎

5.
Global estimates: Proof of Proposition
4.1

In this section, our main goal is to establish the global energy estimate for the reformulated system (
4.6
), i.e., to prove Proposition
4.1
.

5.1.
Estimates for the
d
-equation in (
1.12
)

In this subsection, we estimate separately the zero-frequency part
\bar{d}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}d\,dx
and the nonzero-frequency part
d-\bar{d}
of the fluctuation
d=M-M_{e}
. We also estimate the gradient
\nabla d
in
H^{s}
. First, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer. Then

(5.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\bar{d}|^{2}\lesssim(1+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,,

where the expressions of
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), respectively.

Proof.

Integrating the forth equation of (
4.6
) over
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}d\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}u\cdot\nabla d\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2A\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}(d+M_{e})\,\mathrm{d}x-2A\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(d+M_{e})\times\Delta d\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

A direct computation gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}u\cdot\nabla d\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla\cdot u\,\mathrm{d}x\,\bar{d}=-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
\bar{d}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}d\,\mathrm{d}x
. Moreover,

\displaystyle 2A\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}(d+Me)\,\mathrm{d}x=2A\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+Me+\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

For
1\leq i\leq 3
, using the Einstein summation convention and the Levi-Civita symbol
\varepsilon_{ijk}
(with
\varepsilon_{ijk}=1
for even permutations of
\{123\}
and
-1
for odd permutations), we have

\displaystyle-2A\gamma\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}[(d+Me)\times\Delta d]^{i}\,\mathrm{d}x=-2A\gamma\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varepsilon_{ijk}(d^{j}+M_{e}^{k})\Delta d^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-2A\gamma\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varepsilon_{ijk}(d^{j}+M_{e}^{j})\partial_{l}\partial_{l}d^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x=2A\gamma\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varepsilon_{ijk}\partial_{l}(d^{j}+M_{e}^{j})\partial_{l}d^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2A\gamma\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varepsilon_{ijk}\partial_{l}d^{j}\partial_{l}d^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x=0.

Thus,

-2A\gamma\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(d+M_{e})\times\Delta d\,\mathrm{d}x=0.

Consequently,

\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}d\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x+2A\lambda\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Recalling
\bar{d}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}d\,\mathrm{d}x
. we obtain the equation for the zero-frequency part:

(5.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bar{d}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2A\lambda}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x.

Multiplying (
5.2
) by
\bar{d}
gives

(5.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\bar{d}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{d}}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2A\lambda}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\bar{d}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\mathrm{d}x\,.

By Hölder’s inequality,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{d}}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq|\bar{d}|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\cdot u|\,|d-\bar{d}|\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim|\bar{d}|\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\,\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|\bar{d}|\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\,\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}\,,

where we used the Poincaré inequality
\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}
(since
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(d-\bar{d})\,dx=0
). Furthermore, Hölder’s inequality together with the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
yields

(5.5)

\displaystyle\frac{2A\lambda}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\bar{d}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim|\bar{d}|\,\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left(1+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{\infty}}+|\bar{d}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}+|\bar{d}|\right)|\bar{d}|\,\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

Substituting (
5.4
) and (
5.5
) into (
5.3
), we obtain

(5.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\bar{d}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\bar{d}|\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}+\left(1+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}+|\bar{d}|\right)|\bar{d}|\,\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), estimate (
5.6
) implies (
5.1
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We next estimate the nonzero-frequency part
d-\bar{d}
. More precisely, we establish the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer. Then one has

(5.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,,

where the functionals
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), respectively.

Proof.

It is derived from the difference
\eqref{CMEH-eqGd}_{4}
and (
5.2
) that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(d-\bar{d})+u\cdot\nabla(d-\bar{d})

\displaystyle=2A\lambda\Delta d+2A\lambda|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})

\displaystyle-2A\lambda\left((d-\bar{d})+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\times\Delta d

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\nabla\cdot u)(d-\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\frac{2A\lambda}{|\mathbb{T}^{3}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Multiplying (
5.8
) by
d-\bar{d}
and integrating over
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
, one immediately obtains

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle u\cdot\nabla(d-\bar{d}),d-\bar{d}\right\rangle+2A\lambda\left\langle|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}),d-\bar{d}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-2A\gamma\left\langle(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\times\Delta d,d-\bar{d}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\bar{d}\cdot\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,d-\bar{d}\quad\right\rangle-2A\lambda\bar{d}\cdot\left\langle|\nabla d|^{2},d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right\rangle\,.

The Hölder inequality, the Poincaré inequality
\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}
, and the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
yield

\displaystyle-\left\langle u\cdot\nabla(d-\bar{d}),d-\bar{d}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,|d-\bar{d}|^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}\,,

and

\displaystyle 2A\lambda\left\langle|\nabla d|^{2}(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}),d-\bar{d}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\right)\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,,

and

\displaystyle-\bar{d}\cdot\langle\nabla\cdot u,d-\bar{d}\rangle\lesssim|\bar{d}|\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|\bar{d}|\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}\,,

and

\displaystyle-2A\lambda\bar{d}\cdot\langle|\nabla d|^{2},d-\bar{d}+Me+t\bar{d}\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim|\bar{d}|\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left(\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}+1+|\bar{d}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\right)|\bar{d}|\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

Furthermore, a direct calculation shows the following cancellation:

\displaystyle-2A\gamma\left\langle(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\times\triangle d,d-\bar{d}\right\rangle=-2A\gamma\left\langle(M_{e}+\bar{d})\times\triangle d,d-\bar{d}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2A\gamma\left\langle\varepsilon_{ijk}\left(M_{e}^{j}+\bar{d}^{j}\right)\partial_{l}^{2}d^{k},d^{i}-\bar{d}^{i}\right\rangle=2A\gamma\left\langle\varepsilon_{ijk}\left(M_{e}^{j}+\bar{d}^{j}\right)\partial_{l}d^{k},\partial_{l}d^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2A\gamma\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left\langle(M_{e}+\bar{d})\times\partial_{l}d,\partial_{l}d\right\rangle=0\,.

Collecting all the above relations, one obtains

(5.9)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\right)\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\right)\left(|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\right)\|\nabla d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

We now deal with the higher order derivatives of the nonzero-frequency part
d-\bar{d}
. Applying the derivative operator
\partial^{m}
(
1\leq|m|\leq s
) to (
5.8
) and taking the
L^{2}
inner product of the resulting equation with
\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\left\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[u\cdot\nabla(d-\bar{d})\right],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle

(5.10)

\displaystyle+2A\lambda\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[|\nabla d|^{2}\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\right],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle

\displaystyle-2A\lambda\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\times\nabla d\right],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle.

Since
1\leq|m|\leq s
, we easily have

\displaystyle-\langle\partial^{m}[u\cdot\nabla(d-\bar{d})],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\langle\nabla\cdot u,|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})|^{2}\rangle

Hence,

(5.11)

\displaystyle-\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}(d-\bar{d}),\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{4}}\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\,.

Moreover,

(5.12)

\displaystyle 2A\lambda\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[|\nabla d|^{2}\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\right],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2A\lambda\left\langle\left(\partial^{m}|\nabla d|^{2}\right)\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right),\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle

\displaystyle+2A\lambda\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}|\nabla d|^{2}\,\partial^{m^{\prime}}(d-\bar{d}),\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial^{m}|\nabla d|^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\left\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}\left\|\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}|\nabla d|^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}(d-\bar{d})\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{2}}\right)\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{3}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{4}\,.

Furthermore, using the cancellation
\langle\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\times\Delta d,\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\rangle=0
, we have

\displaystyle-2A\gamma\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\times\Delta d\right],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\underset{II_{1}}{\underbrace{-2A\gamma\left\langle\left(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d}\right)\times\Delta\partial^{m}d,\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle\quad\underset{II_{2}}{\underbrace{-2A\gamma\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}<m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}(d-\bar{d})\times\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}d,\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\rangle}}\,.

For the quantity
II_{1}
,

\displaystyle II_{1}=-2A\gamma\left\langle\varepsilon_{ijk}\left(d^{j}-\bar{d}^{j}+M_{e}^{j}\bar{d}^{j}\right)\Delta\partial^{m}d^{j},\partial^{m}(d^{i}-\bar{d}^{i})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{3}2A\gamma\left\langle\varepsilon_{ijk}\partial_{l}(d^{j}-\bar{d}^{j})\,\partial_{l}\partial^{m}d^{j},\partial^{m}(d^{i}-\bar{d}^{i})\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{3}2A\gamma\left\langle\varepsilon_{ijk}\left(d^{j}-\bar{d}^{j}+M_{e}^{j}+\bar{d}^{j}\right)\partial_{l}\partial^{m}d^{j},\partial_{l}\partial^{m}d^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2A\gamma\sum_{l=1}^{3}\langle\partial_{l}d\times\partial_{l}\partial^{m}d,\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\rangle+2A\gamma\sum_{l=1}^{3}\langle(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\times\partial_{l}\partial^{m}d,\partial_{l}\partial^{m}d\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{3}\,.

The term
II_{2}
can be bounded as

\displaystyle II_{2}\lesssim\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}<m}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{4}}\|\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}d\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{3}\,.

Consequently,

(5.13)

\displaystyle-2A\gamma\langle\partial^{m}\big[(d-\bar{d}+M_{e}+\bar{d})\times\Delta d\big],\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\rangle\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{H^{3}}^{3}\,.

Substituting the estimates (
5.11
), (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) into (
5.1
) yields, for
1\leq|m|\leq s
,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\left\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial^{m}(d-\bar{d})\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\left\|d-\bar{d}\right\|_{H^{s}}\right)\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{3}+\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{4}\,.

Combining (
5.9
) and (
5.14
), we obtain

(5.15)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}\right)\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle+\left(1+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}+|\bar{d}|+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\right)\left(|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\right)\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\,.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), we conclude estimate (
5.7
) from the bound (
5.15
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now we bound the gradient
\nabla d
in the
H^{s}
-space. More precisely, we establish the following lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer. Then one has

(5.16)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+2A\lambda\left\|\Delta d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t))\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,,

where the functionals
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), respectively.

Proof.

We now apply the derivative operator
\partial^{m}
(
|m|\leq s
) to the fourth equation of (
4.6
) and take the
L^{2}
inner product of the resulting equation with
\Delta\partial^{m}d
. This yields

(5.17)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2A\lambda\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla d),\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle-2A\lambda\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[|\nabla d|^{2}(d+Me)\right],\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle+2A\gamma\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[(d+Me)\times\Delta d\right],\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle\,.

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.17
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla d),\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle=\underset{III_{1}}{\underbrace{\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m}d,\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle}}+\underset{III_{2}}{\underbrace{\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}d,\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle}}\,.

The term
III_{1}
can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle III_{1}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\nabla\partial^{m}d,\,\nabla\partial^{m}d\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m}d,\,\nabla\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,\,|\nabla\partial^{m}d|^{2}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m}d,\,\nabla\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

The term
III_{2}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle III_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}d\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Consequently,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla d),\,\nabla\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\,.

The second term on the right-hand side of (
5.17
) can be controlled by

(5.19)

\displaystyle-2A\lambda\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[|\nabla d|^{2}(d+M_{e})\right],\,\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-2A\lambda\left\langle\partial^{m}(|\nabla d|^{2})(d+M_{e}),\,\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle-2A\lambda\mathbf{1}_{|m|\geq 1}\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C^{m^{\prime}}_{m}\left\langle\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}(|\nabla d|^{2})\partial^{m^{\prime}}d,\,\Delta\partial^{m}d\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\|\partial^{m}|\nabla d|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\|\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}|\nabla d|^{2}\|_{L^{4}}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}d\|_{L^{4}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}\right)\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{3}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}.

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\|d-\bar{d}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\right)\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}.

Moreover, using the cancellation
\langle(d+M_{e})\times\Delta\partial^{m}d,\Delta\partial^{m}d\rangle=0
, the last term in (
5.17
) can be bounded by

(5.20)

\displaystyle 2A\gamma\langle\partial^{m}(d+M_{e})\times\Delta d],\Delta\partial^{m}d)=2A\gamma\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}d\times\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}d,\Delta\partial^{m}d\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}d\times\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}d\|_{L^{2}}\,\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla d\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta d\|_{H^{s}}\|\Delta\partial^{m}d\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Substituting the estimates (
5.18
), (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) into (
5.17
) and summing over
|m|\leq s
, we obtain

(5.21)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+2A\lambda\left\|\Delta d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}+\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}\left\|\Delta d\right\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle+\left(1+|\bar{d}|+\left\|d-\bar{d}\right\|_{H^{s}}+\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}\right)\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\left\|\Delta d\right\|_{H^{s}}+\left\|\nabla d\right\|_{H^{s}}\left\|\Delta d\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\,.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), we deduce estimate (
5.16
) from (
5.21
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.2.
Estimates for the
\theta
-equation in (
4.6
)

The main goal of this subsection is to derive the estimate for
\theta
in the weighted space
H^{s}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}
, where the weighted function
\mathfrak{w}(\theta)
is defined in (
4.12
). More precisely, we establish the following lemma.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer. Then one has

(5.22)

\displaystyle\frac{a\gamma}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{\mathfrak{w}(\theta)}}-\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle\lesssim\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,,

where the functionals
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), respectively.

Proof.

Taking the
L^{2}
inner product of the first equation in (
4.6
) with
\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\theta
yields

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\theta,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\theta\rangle+\langle u\cdot\nabla\theta,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\theta\rangle+\langle(1+\theta)\nabla\cdot u,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\theta\rangle=0\,.

The first term on the left-hand side can be computed as

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\theta,\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta},\partial_{t}\frac{1}{2}\theta^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}|\theta|^{2}\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)\theta^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\theta\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right),\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle\,.

The second term on the left-hand side can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\theta,\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\theta^{2},\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle u\cdot\nabla,\ \theta^{2}\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right),\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle\,.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)+u\cdot\nabla\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right),\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,\ \theta^{2}\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,\ P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\underset{IV_{1}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)+\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}u\right),\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle}}\ \underset{IV_{2}}{\underbrace{-\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,\ P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\theta\right\rangle}}\,.

The term
IV_{1}
can be estimated as

\displaystyle IV_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)^{\prime}(\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla\theta),\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\nabla\cdot u,\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle-(1+\theta)\nabla\cdot u\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\nabla\cdot u,\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}-\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)^{\prime}\right)\nabla\cdot u,\ \theta^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{L^{2}}\|\theta\|^{2}_{L^{4}}\,.

where we have used the fact
0<c_{0}\leq 1+\theta\leq c_{1}
established in Theorem
1.1
. By the Sobolev embedding theorem
\|\theta\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\|\theta\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\theta\|^{\frac{3}{4}}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\theta\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H^{1}}
, and thus

(5.23)

\displaystyle IV_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta\|_{H^{1}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\theta\|_{H^{1}}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}})\,.

Furthermore, the term
IV_{2}
can be bounded by

(5.24)

\displaystyle IV_{2}

\displaystyle=-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\cdot u,\theta\rangle-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle+\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle P^{\prime\prime}(1+\theta)\theta\nabla\theta,u\rangle+\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{4}}+\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}+\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta\|_{H^{1}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}})\left(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle\,.

Consequently, one obtains

(5.25)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta,u\rangle\lesssim(\|\theta\|_{H^{1}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}})(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}})\,.

Next we deal with the higher order derivatives. Applying the derivative operator
\partial^{m}
(
1\leq|m|\leq s
) to the first equation of (
4.6
) and taking the
L^{2}
inner product of the resulting equation with
\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta
, we obtain

(5.26)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\partial^{m}\theta,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle+\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla\theta),\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\partial^{m}[(1+\theta)\nabla\cdot u],\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle=0\,.

It is easy to see that

(5.27)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\partial^{m}\theta,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta},\partial_{t}\frac{1}{2}|\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right),|\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\right\rangle\,,

and

(5.28)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla\theta),\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C^{m^{\prime}}_{m}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta,\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\cdot u,\ |\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right),\ |\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C^{m^{\prime}}_{m}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta,\ \frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\right\rangle\,,

and

(5.29)

\displaystyle\langle\partial^{m}[(1+\theta)\nabla\cdot u],\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}u,P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\partial^{m}\theta\rangle+\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}\theta\nabla\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\rangle

\displaystyle=-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle+\langle\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}u,P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\partial^{m}\theta\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}\theta\nabla\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\rangle\,.

Substituting (
5.27
), (
5.28
) and (
5.29
) into (
5.26
) yields

(5.30)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle

\displaystyle=\underset{VI_{1}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partial_{t}(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta})+\nabla\cdot\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)u,|\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle\underset{VI_{2}}{\underbrace{-\langle\nabla\cdot\partial^{m}u,P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\partial^{m}\theta\rangle-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle}}

\displaystyle\underset{VI_{3}}{\underbrace{-\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\rangle}}

\displaystyle\underset{VI_{4}}{\underbrace{-\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}\theta\nabla\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\partial^{m}\theta\rangle}}\,,

By the same arguments as in (
5.23
), we have

\displaystyle VI_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)^{\prime}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta+\nabla\cdot u\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta},|\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\right\rangle

(5.31)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}-(1+\theta)\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)^{\prime}\right)\nabla\cdot u,|\partial^{m}\theta|^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\,,

Analogous to the estimates in (
5.24
), it follows that

(5.32)

\displaystyle VI_{2}

\displaystyle=\langle\partial^{m}u,P^{\prime\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta\partial^{m}\theta\rangle\lesssim\|P^{\prime\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\theta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\,,

Using the fact
|\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}|\lesssim 1
, we obtain

(5.33)

\displaystyle VI_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C^{m^{\prime}}_{m}\langle|\partial^{m^{\prime}}u|\cdot|\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta|,|\partial^{m}\theta|\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{4}}\cdot\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}<m}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\|_{L^{4}}\cdot\|\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta\|_{L^{4}}\cdot\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\cdot\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}\cdot\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\,,

Applying similar estimates as in (
5.32
), we have

(5.34)

\displaystyle VI_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}<m}\|\partial^{m^{\prime}}\theta\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\cdot\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u\|_{L^{4}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\,,

Therefore, inserting the estimates (
5.2
), (
5.32
), (
5.33
) and (
5.34
) into (
5.29
) yields, for all
1\leq|m|\leq s
,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}-\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\partial^{m}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\,,

Combining (
5.25
) and (
5.35
), we obtain

(5.36)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}}}-\sum_{|m|\leq s}\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}+\|u\|_{H^{s}}\right)\left(\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}\right)\,.

Notice that
a\gamma\mathfrak{w}(\theta)=\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}
. Recalling the definitions of
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), we deduce estimate (
5.22
) from (
5.36
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.3.
Estimates for the
u
-equation in (
4.6
)

In this subsection, we aim to establish an estimate for the velocity
u
in the
H^{s}_{1+\theta}
norm. More precisely, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
s\geq 3
be an integer. Then one has

(5.37)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|u\|_{H_{1+\theta}^{s}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+(\mu+\xi)\|\nabla\cdot u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{|m|\leq s}\left\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\right\rangle-\sum_{|m|\leq s}\left\langle\nabla\partial^{m}\psi,\nabla\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{s}{2}}(t))\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)\,,

where the functionals
\mathbf{E}_{s}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}_{s}(t)
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), respectively.

Proof.

We first rewrite the second
u
-equation in (
4.6
) as follows:

(5.38)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u+\frac{1}{1+\theta}\nabla\left(P(1+\theta)-A|\nabla d|^{2}+\tilde{g}(U)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu}{1+\theta}\Delta u+\frac{\mu+\xi}{1+\theta}\nabla(\nabla\cdot u)-\Delta\psi-\frac{2}{1+\theta}\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta

\displaystyle-\frac{\theta}{1+\theta}\nabla\left(\theta+\tilde{g}(U)\right)-\frac{2A}{1+\theta}\nabla\cdot\left(\nabla d\odot\nabla d\right)\,.

Applying the derivative operator
\partial^{m}\ (|m|\leq s)
to (
5.38
) and taking the
L^{2}
inner product with
\nabla\partial^{m}\theta
, we obtain

(5.39)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\partial^{m}u\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\theta}}^{2}+\underset{A_{1}}{\underbrace{\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla u),\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle+\underset{A_{2}}{\underbrace{\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\nabla\mathcal{P}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}+\underset{A_{3}}{\underbrace{\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\tilde{g}(U)-A|\nabla d|^{2}}{1+\theta}\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{4}}{\underbrace{\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\mu}{1+\theta}\Delta u\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}+\underset{A_{5}}{\underbrace{\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\mu+\xi}{1+\theta}\nabla(\nabla\cdot u)\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle\underset{A_{6}}{\underbrace{-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{2}{1+\theta}\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla\theta\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}\underset{A_{7}}{\underbrace{-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\theta}{1+\theta}\nabla\left(\theta+\tilde{g}(U)\right)\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle\underset{A_{8}}{\underbrace{-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{2A}{1+\theta}\nabla\cdot(\nabla d\odot\nabla d)\right],\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}\underset{A_{9}}{\underbrace{-\left\langle\Delta\partial^{m}\psi,\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}\,.

The term
A_{1}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-A_{1}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla u),\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m}u,\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle u\cdot\nabla|\partial^{m}u|^{2},\ 1+\theta\right\rangle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\underset{A_{11}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\cdot u\ |\partial^{m}u|^{2},\ 1+\theta\right\rangle}}+\underset{A_{12}}{\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\left\langle u\cdot\nabla\theta,\ |\partial^{m}u|^{2}\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle\underset{A_{13}}{\underbrace{-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}u\cdot\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}\,.

For
A_{11}
, we have

\displaystyle{A_{11}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{3}}\|\partial^{m}u\|^{2}_{L^{3}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\,,

where we used the Sobolev embedding
\|u\|_{L^{3}}\lesssim\|u\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}
. Similarly,
A_{12}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle{A_{12}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{4}}\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}^{2})(\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2})\,,

where we used the inequality
\|u\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\|u\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|^{\frac{3}{4}}_{L^{2}}
. Moreover,
A_{13}
can be estimated analogously:

\displaystyle{A_{13}}\lesssim\sum_{0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m}\langle|\partial^{m^{\prime}}u||\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u|,(1+\theta)|\partial^{m}u|\rangle\lesssim\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\,.

Consequently,
A_{1}
satisfies

(5.40)

\displaystyle-A_{1}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial^{m}(u\cdot\nabla u),\ (1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle\lesssim(\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}})(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla\theta\|^{2}_{H^{s}})\,.

For the term
A_{2}
, we compute

\displaystyle-A_{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\nabla P(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right],(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle=-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\nabla\theta\right],(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\right\rangle\underset{A_{21}}{\underbrace{-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}\left[\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right]\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta,(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}\,.

By Lemma 3.2 of
[
11
]
, we have

\displaystyle A_{21}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}\left\langle|\partial^{m^{\prime}}\left[\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right]||\nabla\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\theta|,|\partial^{m}u|\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\left(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+\theta)}{1+\theta}\right)\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left\|\nabla\theta\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}^{s}\right)\left\|\nabla\theta\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}+\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}^{s}\right)\left\|\nabla\theta\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}\,.

Hence,

(5.41)

\displaystyle-A_{2}+\left\langle P^{\prime}(1+\theta)\nabla\partial^{m}\theta,\partial^{m}u\right\rangle\lesssim\left(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}+\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}^{s}\right)\left\|\nabla\theta\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}\,.

For the term
A_{3}
, we note that

\displaystyle-A_{3}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\tilde{g}(U)-A|\nabla d|^{2}}{1+\theta}\right],(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\tilde{g}(U)-A|\nabla d|^{2}\right],\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}_{A_{31}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\theta}\right)\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}\left[\tilde{g}(U)-A|\nabla d|^{2}\right],\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}_{A_{32}}\,.

Since
\tilde{g}(U)=O(|U|^{2})
and
U=\nabla\psi
, we have

\displaystyle A_{31}\lesssim\left(\|\tilde{g}(U)\|_{H^{s}}+\||\nabla d|^{2}\|_{H^{s}}\right)\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\||\nabla d|^{2}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\right)\|\partial^{m}u\|_{L^{2}}\,,

and

\displaystyle A_{32}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}\left\langle\left|\partial^{m^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\theta}\right)\right|\left|\partial^{m}\left[\tilde{g}(U)-A|\nabla d|^{2}\right]\right|,|\partial^{m}u|\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\left(\frac{1}{1+\theta}\right)\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left(\|\tilde{g}(U)\|_{H^{s}}+\||\nabla d|^{2}\|_{H^{s}}\right)\|\nabla u\|_{H^{s-1}}\,.

Applying Lemma 3.2 of
[
11
]
and the fact
\|\frac{1}{1+\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla(\frac{1}{1+\theta})\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|^{s}_{H^{s-1}}\,.

Then

\displaystyle A_{32}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\nabla\theta\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|^{s}_{H^{s-1}})(\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|^{2}_{H^{s}})\|u\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta\|_{H^{s}}+\|\theta\|^{s}_{H^{s}})(\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|^{2}_{H^{s}})\|u\|_{H^{s}}\,.

Therefore,

(5.42)

\displaystyle-A_{3}\lesssim(1+\|\theta\|^{s}_{H^{s}})(\|\nabla\psi\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\nabla d\|^{2}_{H^{s}})\|u\|_{H^{s}}\,.

We now estimate the term
A_{4}
. Observe that

\displaystyle A_{4}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\mu}{1+\theta}\Delta u\right],(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mu\left\langle\Delta\partial^{m}u,\partial^{m}u\right\rangle+\underset{A_{41}}{\underbrace{\mu\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}C_{m}^{m^{\prime}}\left\langle\partial^{m^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\theta}\right)\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u,(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle}}\,.

It is easy to see that

\mu\left\langle\Delta\partial^{m}u,\partial^{m}u\right\rangle=-\mu\left\|\nabla\partial^{m}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,,

and

(5.43)

\displaystyle A_{41}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\neq m^{\prime}\leq m\end{subarray}}\left\langle\left|\partial^{m^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\theta}\right)\right|\left|\Delta\partial^{m-m^{\prime}}u\right|,\left|\partial^{m}u\right|\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\left(\frac{1}{1+\theta}\right)\right\|_{H^{s-1}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\lesssim\left(1+\left\|\theta\right\|_{H^{s}}^{s-1}\right)\left\|\theta\right\|_{H^{s}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\,.

Consequently,

(5.44)

\displaystyle\mu\left\|\nabla\partial^{m}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+A_{4}\lesssim\left(1+\left\|\theta\right\|_{H^{s}}^{s-1}\right)\left\|\theta\right\|_{H^{s}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\,.

For the term
A_{5}
,

\displaystyle A_{5}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\partial^{m}\left[\frac{\mu+\xi}{1+\theta}\nabla(\nabla\cdot u)\right],(1+\theta)\partial^{m}u\right\rangle
